TIO N. MEETING AT WARRINGTON.

VISIT TO MESSRS. FLETCHER, RUSSELL AD

CO'S. WORKS:

Great interest was manifested in the visit to Warring- ton,on Saturday, of the members of the Midland Coun- ties Branch of the British Dental Association,representa- tives being present from Bolton and district,Manchester, York, Liverpool, Newcastle, Bangor, Leeds, Bradford, Eccles,Oldham, Rochdale,Bury, Halifax, Dowsbury,and many other large centres of population, the company numbering about 150, including the students from the IManchester and Liverpool Dental Hospitals. Warring- ton had been selected for the spring meeting, and Mr. Thomas Fletcher, F.C.S. (of the firm of Messrs. Fletcher, IRussell, and Co., the eminent gas engineers and artistic ironfounders of Warrington, Manchester, and London), and Mr. John Taylor, L.D.S.I. (Warrington),invited the visitors to partake of their liberal hospitality. On arri- val the party made a tour of inspection of Messrs. Fletcher's Warrington works, being conducted by Mr. Fletcher and Mr. Ernest L. Fletcher, who displayed the greatest interest in their guests' comfort and pleasure. The visitors were filled with admiration as they passed from room to room, and witnessed the marvellous rapi- dity and perfection with which workmen produced specialities for which the firm are so noted. Among ob- jects of interest inspected were domestic cooking,water heating, and laundry appliances, boiling burners, coffee roasters, gas governors, gas fires, stoves, greenhouse boilers,and general heating apparatu,; workshop, labor- atory and trade appliances for gas, gasoline, petroleum, and oil gas, and furnaces aad blow pipes of the most ingenious kind. The party naturally examined with curiosity and plea- sure the dental appliances and the novel furnaces for gold &1., melting. The effective nature of the furnaces was demonstrated, and an oxygen blow pipe pierced a flame through a bar of cast iron and also steel in a very limited time. Mr. Fletcher explaining that he could drive it through any safe. The rapid heating of bars was also shown, and it was stated that the firm's Intents were being largely used in the South African goldfields. three pinto of crude petroleum being sufficient to heat 300 inch mining bars in an hour. Prior to leaving, the party ex- pressed thanks for the pleasure afforded by the visit,and learned with much interest that the firm were erecting a new and colossal works at Wilderspool on the most modern lines, the motive power being electricity, and the casting and enamelling done by secret processes. An adjournment was afterwards made to the Assembly Room of the Lion Hotel, where a splendid repast was served in Mrs. Gilbert's best style. During tea a selec- tion of tasteful music was given by Mr. A. It. Sutton. The president, Mr. I. Renshaw, L.D.S. (Rochdale) occu- pied the chair subsequently, and wee supported by Mr. Fletcher,Mr.Taylor,Mr. Ernest L.Fletcher,Mr. W.Fletcher. Dr. Fox, Dr. Jcsepif' Dr. Adams, Dr.Langdale, Dr. Lead- better, Mr. A.Neil, Mr. J. Neil, Mr, T. E. King, L.D.S. (York), hon. sec. Mr. G.F. Campion.L.D.S.(Menchester) bon. treasurer, and other guests. The CHAIRMAN warmly thanked Messrs. Fletcher and Taylor for their hospitality, and slid there were no two men in the profession or connected with it whom they respected more. (Hear, beer.) Although Mr. Fletcher was not a member of the British Dental Asseciation,and was not an active member of their profession, twelve or eighteen months ago they did him the honour to elect him an honorary associate of their branch. (Applause.) Mr. Fletcher had done a great deal towards elevating the status of dentistry, and had made apparatus of such a character that it was a difficult matter to do bad work. (Laughter and hear, hear.) He not only made filling materials tin y used in their surgeries, but had improved their workroom appliance to such an extent that it was a pleasure to work with them. For the improvements I his services to the profession, and his uniform kindness they were deeply indebted to him. (Hear, hear.) He concluded by proposing a hearty vote ot thanks to Messrs Fletcher and Taylor.

Dr. WAITE (Liverpool) seconded, remarking that that was a unique gathering of past, present, and future members. Past and present were represented by prac- titioners from all parts, and the future by the students from the Dental Hospital. Ha would like to tell the meeting that Messrs. Fletcher and Taylor had not only invited the students from Liverpool and Manchester,but had given each a free railway pass. (Applause.) This thoughtful consideration and genuine liberality would fix the meeting indelibly in the hearts and minds of all present, and would be an incentive and inspiration. The resolution was heartily passed, and was accorded musical honours. Mr. TAYLOR responded, and on behalf of Mr. Fletcher and himself gave all a hearty welcome. He added that he had been really acting as the medium to see that the gathering was carried through. One of the pleasantest aspects of Mr. Fletcher's life was to keep in touch with the dental profession, (Applause.) They acknowledged him as an authority in dental matters, and to his skill and genius,rnore particularly as regarded the workroom, they were greatly indebted. I Mr. FLETCHER also briefly and appropriately returned thanks. He afterwards read a paper on "How to suc- ceed." After a humorous reference to his early medical and surgical education, he addressed himself to the students, and said the foundation of reputation and position was laid at an earlier date than was generally supposed. He next made an announcement which was received with loud applause. For several years, he said, there had been a series of prizes in his name at the Man- chester Dental School, and the spirit of emulation in regard to these had been so often spoken about that he was arranging for a similar series at the Liverpool School. In Manchester they had proved an unqualified success, and this would no doubt be the case in Liver- pool also. (Hear, hear.) Messrs. C. Ash and Sons (London) had offered to practically increase the gift by • supplying instruments and tools at a greatly reduced price for the prizes, and the amount between the two schools, if taken iii Instruments and tools, would be well over £5O per annum. There was no condition attached to the prizes except that they should be applied to those branches where the Deans and Ceuncils of the schools considered they would be of special encouragement. (Applause.) Proceeding, he assured the students that the first and most important help to success was that their occupation should be congenial and an amusement to them. Opportunities came to all. The way up the ladder was to get out of the crowd at the bottom, build their own steps up, where there was more room, plea- sure, and comfort. It was not enough to do as well as other people. This only left them in the outside crowd, A man might be a general failure, but if he learned to do something better than anyone else he might become a great success by using his special knowledge. (Hear. hear.) The day when they would all be made equal and valued at so much per dozen, like eggs, was not yet upon them, and men then they must remain the makers of their own fortunes. (Applause.) Mr. H. T. DRESCHFELD, L.D.5.1., next gave a demon- stration on " Gold and amalgam crowns," Mr. J. S. Dickin, L.D.S.I. (Southport) exhibited a cataphoric ap- paratus and explained its working and arrangements. and Messrs. J. Taylor, J. A. Woods, L.D.L. Eng., and G. Brunton also gave demonstrations. Mr. Taylor greatly interested his fellow-dentists, by explaining the successful replantation of a tooth in his son's mouth after it had been knocked out by a cricket ball. He stated that in May last lais son had one of hie front teeth knocked out while playing cricket, and came home three hours and a half afterwards with the tooth in his pocket. The cavity was at once carefully syringed and the tooth replaced with strong silk ligatures. It remained so for six weeks when the result was found to be most satisfactory. The tooth was examined by many of those present, and the Presi- dent pronannced it to be about the most successful ease of replantation he had ever seen.—Profitable discuasions r ensued on the various subjects, and the visit will rank ' ite one of the most enieyable and successful in the his- tory of the association.
